# Data loss register

This register lists every step that changes or drops source content between the raw API responses and any derived table, with its cost and reason. `CLAUDE.md`, "Data loss register", requires a row for each step, covering attendance, persons, memberships, sittings, report titles, and hand overrides in `data/`, as well as markdown and speech rows. Update a row in the same change that moves its figure.

## Principle

The raw API responses are stored exactly as received and are never rewritten: `HansardWebsiteResponse.content`, `Report.content`, and the raw sitting table. Everything else is derived and can be rebuilt from them: markdown, speech rows, and attendance. A transformation in this register therefore loses nothing for good. It loses it from the derived layer, and the parser can bring it back from the raw HTML.

Two things are not recoverable from the raw layer alone, and each says so below:

- Row ids. A write that deletes a speech row removes its id. The previous rows live in the `pg_dump` taken before each loop write. Those dumps sit outside git.
- Two fields the newer system serves are never stored. See "Exceptions to raw first".

## How to read the register

- **Cost** is the count the records give. "Not measured" means no file checked for the register holds a count. The loop's counts come from dry runs and from the replica; where a figure is an estimate, the cell says so.
- **Reversible** is "yes" when re-running the parser on the raw HTML regains the content. A write that replaced stored rows is also undone by restoring the dump taken before it.
- **Stored rows** says whether the change reached the database. The combined write (fixes F7, F3, F6 and F2, written together), write #2, write #3, write #4, and write #5 are in the stored rows. Write #4 is the three parser fixes of cycle 4 and the release of 42 held reports. Write #5 is the w5 parser patches 01a to 09 and the stage 8 links of patch 05.
- **Decided** holds "Maintainers" or "No recorded decision", with any commit, ADR, report, or decision id in brackets. "No recorded decision" means a commit or doc made the change and the records show no decision by the maintainers. When an agent made the call for the maintainers during an unattended run (an independent decider or an overnight run), the brackets name it. A cell that records several decisions labels each part.
- A query id in a Cost cell, such as C1a, names a query in the closing section, "Queries for the measurements of 26 October 2026". A figure from a parse run in memory, with a rule switched off or counted as it fires, says so in its cell; the scripts for those runs are kept outside the repository.

| Character references in older-system HTML keep their characters: `&ndash;` is "–", `&rsquo;` is "’", `&ldquo;` is "“", `&eacute;` is "é". Before, html2text ran with `unicode_snob` off and wrote each as its ASCII fold ("-", "'", '"', "e", and "--" for `&mdash;`), so stored markdown and speech lost the source character. The no-break space becomes a space (row above). Title matching and the space html2text puts before an emphasis mark still read a kept character by its ASCII fold, so no report starts at another line and no spacing moves. | `utils/markdown_parser.py`: `_SourceCharacterConverter`, `ASCII_FOLDED_CHARACTERS`; `services/speech.py`: `_is_line_matching_title`, `_ADJOURNMENT_MOVER_RE` | Dashes read as hyphens and curly quotes as straight ones, and "Café" was stored as "Cafe" (data sanity persona pass 2, DS2-02). | Each older-system speech row stores the printed column it starts in (`speech.column_start`), and each report its column range (`report.column_start`, `report.column_end`), read from the column markers in the raw HTML. A row gets NULL where the markers do not fix its column: it starts between markers that skip a number, after the last marker when the head's `End_Col` is past that marker, in a report whose markers step back or jump by more than 50, before the first marker when the head's `Start_Col` is not that marker minus one, or its text is not found in the HTML. A report gets no range when its markers step back or jump by more than 50, or when the head's `End_Col` is more than one past the last marker, because nothing printed then fixes where it ends. | Labels that are not people stop being speakers (memo 01a B and E, finding DS-05). (1) A bold phrase that opens with a member's name and a verb or more than one lowercase word ("Dr Wang Kai Yuen asked yesterday:") is part of the current speaker's speech, text kept whole. (2) A bold heading with no text, ending in a colon, or followed by "(Cont.)", or a bold heading with a subtitle on a markdown heading line, becomes a HEADING row with no speaker. (3) A bold "Question" at the head of a procedural line and the "To ask" of a numbered question stub become rows with no speaker. (4) A bold cell in a line holding a pipe stays with the current speaker, unless the bold run is a person's label with a colon and every pipe comes after it ("**Mr Ong Ye Kung** : ... AHPC | Announcements"), which stays a label. (5) A bracketed chair annotation split across bold runs ("[Deputy Speaker (Mr Charles Chong) in the Chair]") sets no speaker and its text is dropped, as the unsplit form already was. | `report_asker` stores one row per asker of each parliamentary question report (oral-answer, written-answer, written-answer-na), using five rules: an asked row (a row that opens "My question is (simply) to ask" counts as one when the Speaker's last chair row before it gave leave for "a question without notice": report 35665), a To-ask row before any asked row, a line saying the question stood in a member's name, a member's name printed in a row with no speaker label ("Assoc. Prof " with a dot and no second dot is a title there: report 27839, and the name goes to the resolver with the second dot added, so it links as the member's own label does while `printed_name` keeps the record's form), and, only when none of those finds an asker, a chair row whose whole text names a member and calls the question ("Mr Marshall, Question No. 22." or "Question. Mr Marshall.") when the next row is a speech by a member with a speaker_id whose label has the same title and surname (source `CHAIR_CALL`, printed name as the chair prints it: reports 38885, 39076). For a member found repeatedly in a report, only the first row stays. A printed name that resolves to no single Membership keeps the name and no `speaker_id`, and the site shows no asker for it. A stood line that is the report's last paragraph and holds no question credits no one (4 credits dropped, reports 30101, 30105, 30117, 30873). A report whose title starts "Questions for oral answer stood over" credits no one (report 42200, 10 credits dropped). An asked row credits no one when every row with a speaker carries the asker's speaker id (two or more such rows), and no later row opens with a printed label such as "Prof. S. Jayakumar:" or calls a question ("Mr Speaker, Sir, Question 1.", which shows the lone speaker is the asker), because the record then prints the answerer's name in the asker's place (report 20181, 1 credit dropped; of 7,706 reports with one speaker id, 4 more label every row and keep their asker inline, 26319, 27255, 30635, 37816). A stood line also takes the footnote form "Question No. 5* stood in the name of Mr Ong Chang Sam." (the marker after the number: report 38367, 1 credit gained). A marker before the number credits no one, because in 38368 those lines belong to questions the first row says were postponed (2 credits dropped). A To-ask question printed inside a Speaker row after footnote marks and a number ("*2. Mr Tee Kim Leng: To ask the Prime Minister ...") credits that member, source `NAME_IN_TEXT` (report 38412, 3 credits gained). A stood line also records the member who read the question out, from a numbered read-out ("Question No. 5, Sir.") or a line naming the absent member ("in the absence of Mr P. Govindaswamy, may I ask Question No. 1?"). Credited to no one: a stood line that prints no name, a To-ask row printed after an asked row (a question that stood over or was postponed, printed under the last question answered), and a question report no rule matches. The member who reads out a To-ask filer's question is recorded as its reader: 50 of the 97 To-ask rows that still credit a member gain one (a numbered row takes the next read-out of its number, an unnumbered row the first read-out before the next asked or To-ask row, and a read-out may open "Mr Deputy Speaker, Sir,", which gains 32508). When two or more unnumbered To-ask rows come right after one another before one read-out, the record does not say whose question was read, so none of them gets a reader (report 22632: 1 reader not recorded, which B4 had given the last row of the block), unless the chair row just before the read-out calls one block member by name ("Dr Aline Wong."), when that member gets the reader (report 42459: 1 reader gained, 1 not recorded). The other 47 have no read-out found. An asked row also gains a reader when the next row, past any chair row, is another member's read-out of a question number ("Question No. 11, Sir.", or a label "(in the absence of Mr Lee Kuan Yew)"), the row before it is not an asked or To-ask row, and the reader is not the filer under another printed name: 4 of the 34,205 asked rows (27568, 27665, 35260, 41535). Not recorded: 24068, where three asked rows by two members come before one "Question No. 1, Sir.", and 27802, where the filer reads out his own question under another printed name. | `services/report_asker.py`; `populate/report_askers.py`; `scripts/apply_report_askers.py`; `populate/refresh_runs.py`; migration `52f4f7b77f1f` | Readers can find each question's asker and each member's questions. A wrong link is worse than no link (ADR 0002), so an unresolved name stays unlinked. No other report changes. Round 3 (run at `a8f6377`): 34,829 rows in 31,802 reports, 31,801 of them with a resolved asker. Against the 4 November run, 7 rows are added (27839, 35665, 38367, 38368 twice, 38885, 39076), 1 is removed (20181), and the asker in 42459 gains its reader. Counted on 4 November 2026 (dry run, 31,970 question reports): 34,823 rows, 14 fewer than the 34,837 stored (counted on 3 November). A. | `report_participant` stores one row per person and role in each parliamentary question report (oral-answer, written-answer, written-answer-na): asker, absent filer, reader, asker's follow-up, supplementary, answerer, chair and unlabelled. `report_question_state` stores one row per state the record prints: not a question, not asked aloud, a correction (an addendum title, or a paragraph that prints "supplied the following answer", "additional answer" or "information" without citing a Standing Order) and urgent (a Private Notice). The asker, absent filer and reader rows repeat `report_asker`, and the pass blocks a commit if they differ. A second member who calls the same question number after the stored reader and before the answer is also a reader, in this pass only, with role source `QUESTION_NUMBER_CALL` (30999); `report_asker` keeps one reader per filer, so the check leaves these rows out. A member who is not an asker and calls "Question No. N" before the answer is a reader with that source too, when the report's askers have no reader (22632, 24068) or, with no asker, the answer says "through his proxy" (30117). A filer whose question has a reader in `report_asker`, from any rule (a stood line, a To-ask row, an asked row), is an absent filer, and the reader takes the reader role; the pass refuses to commit if a `report_asker` row with a reader does not map to an absent filer. A member who reads out "Question No. N" where no reader is recorded for the question (several unnumbered rows before one read-out, a number misprinted as "B" in 34473, a call of the next question after the answer) is unlabelled, as are "Some hon. Members", a member who speaks before the answer, and the question rows of another question printed in the report. A person who holds any other role in the report has no unlabelled row. A member who is not an asker, and whose only paragraphs after the answer are points of order or interjections of 4 words or fewer that neither end in "?" nor ask, is unlabelled, not supplementary; comments and points of information stay supplementary. A paragraph of 6 words or fewer straight after a chair row that calls "Question No N" belongs to the called question, not this one (43982). A paragraph of 30 words or fewer whose last sentence is a call of a question number ("Question No. 5, Sir", "Question No 5, please", "Question 11") and that asks nothing is also a question row; a paragraph that only mentions a number is not, unless the speaker is the asker or on the chair or holds a government office, so a member who calls the next question mid-paragraph is unlabelled, not supplementary. A paragraph that opens with a name and a colon ("Prof. S. Jayakumar:", "Dr Toh Chin Chye :", "The Second Minister for Defence (Policy)(Dr Yeo Ning Hong)(for ...):") and whose name resolves to a member other than the printed speaker is read, in this pass only, as that member's paragraph, with the inline text as `printed_name` and as the label for the office; a row printed under a chair label is left alone. Text inside a paragraph that runs on after a full stop into "The <office> (<name>):" starts that member's paragraph when the name resolves (26932). A label whose for-bracket is nested in the name bracket ("(Dr John Chen Seow Phun (for the Minister for National Development))") is read for the inner name, and when that resolves to a member with paragraphs in the report the two are one person, with the bracket stored as printed (25916, 31140, 42915). The `speech` rows stay unchanged. The paragraphs that follow it under the same printed label belong to that member too, until a paragraph with another label or a new inline name. A label prints the office only in an oral answer, so the office comes from the first source that has one: this report's labels, its MPs Speaking header, the sitting's attendance list, another label that day, and, for a member the attendance list does not name, his most common label in that Parliament's questions. The office is kept as printed, with a coarse class beside it that takes the highest office a two-office text names. The class and the government-office test read a copy of the text with a space put back where a lowercase letter runs into Minister, Ministry, Senior, Parliamentary, Secretary or State (report 23607 prints "SeniorMinister of State, ... andMinistry of Health"); the stored text stays as printed. Only that report's office is affected. A chair paragraph whose label resolves to no member takes the office its label prints, and the person stays unlinked (71 rows). A row whose label prints a government office, or whose speaker holds one that day, is never read as a read-out question row, and a question row by an answerer gets no unlabelled row (a minister's opening line such as "Question No 2 on today's Order Paper ... is also relevant"). An office holder who speaks after the first answerer is an answerer only if one run of their consecutive paragraphs answers: a point of order or clarification, a motion to extend Question Time, "may I reply", a question or supplementary they ask, an exclamation of 6 words or fewer, a question of 8 words or fewer, and a reply of 6 words or fewer straight after a chair row do not. Beyond those, a run answers only when it holds a paragraph of 12 words or more, or a bare "Yes.", "No." or "Correct." straight after a member's paragraph, and none of its paragraphs says "not supposed to answer", "reply on my behalf", "leave that (entirely) to you" or "allowing me to join". A run that opens on "my previous reply" or "the earlier clarification" does not answer. This loses brief real answers by a later holder as well as the quips: 38510, 38647, 38893 and 42033 are unlabelled (a rule a reader can check, with no quip stored as an answer). A question put to a member ("could the Member", "ask a clarification of", a sentence naming the Leader of the Opposition that ends in "?") is asking. An office whose only source is the Parliament guess stays as a mark but makes nobody an answerer (33485). Only a run before the holder's first question counts: once a later office holder has asked a member something, what they say next carries on that exchange, so Ms Indranee Rajah and Mr Teo Chee Hean in 9432, who ask the Leader of the Opposition and then argue with his reply, are supplementary. A holder with no answering run is dropped from answerer and takes supplementary when a paragraph after the first answer asks something, and unlabelled otherwise; a dropped holder never sets the first answer. A report titled "Questions for oral answer stood over" holds no participant rows, because nobody asked or answered that day (report 42200: its 9 unlabelled rows and its position answerer are removed). A report with no asker and either a non-question title or no question row holds no participant rows. A report with no speech rows holds no state (15040, 22165, 24298), and a report that prints only a heading keeps not a question. A paragraph that says the minister "was absent when the question was called" gives not asked aloud, not a correction, and counts as a question row (39956). `written-answer-na` stays in `report.report_type` and is not copied as a state. Urgent comes from "(by Private Notice", or from "question without notice" with the Speaker's leave (Standing Order 19 or "urgent"), so it holds 2: 35665 joins. | `services/report_participant.py`; `services/report_asker.py`: `get_question_row_ordinals`; `utils/office.py`; `services/attendance.py`: `get_entry_office`, `get_sitting_entry_offices`; `populate/report_participants.py`; `scripts/apply_report_participants.py`; `populate/refresh_runs.py`; migrations `b50219e27a8f`, `287f3a4e2fdd` | Readers can find who asked, answered, and followed up, and for which minister. States after the run: not a question 150, not asked aloud 59, correction 11, urgent 1; 39 questions with an asker and no answerer print no state. A correction wins over not a question, so a report with an addendum title or such a paragraph keeps its question rows and gets the correction: 7 reports move from not a question (38206, 39437, 39517, 39956, 40924, 41992, 32219) and 2 more gain it beside their other states (40245, 41781), measured on the 25 question reports that print the phrase. | A SPEECH row containing "There are" (or a sibling phrase) and the word "ayes" but gives no second count makes no division and goes to the review list. A row that begins with a division opening but prints no counts makes no division, and so does a printed line with a digit or "Nil" left after the counts read, because an unknown label would otherwise drop the counts after it. | `services/division.py`: `get_report_divisions`, `_TRAILING_COUNT_PATTERN` | The parser never guesses a number from "the rest" (Clarification 7 of `plans/votes-and-minutes.md`), and one vote must not become two divisions (Clarification 23). | Not measured. | A minutes list ends at the first line that holds a digit or a quotation mark, a cell of more than six words, or a lone cell that ends in a full stop after a word of three letters or more. All 4 lists move from COUNTS_DISAGREE to STORED, and no STORED list changes. The limits of 5 and 8 spaces were set on these lists, and the 37 lists stored before hold no pair that they join. | A minutes list that cannot be read, or whose side counts differ from the printed counts, gets no vote rows for that division. A list whose first data row holds fewer names than the header has labels gives those names the header's leftmost labels in order, and is LAYOUT_UNREADABLE unless the sides with a printed count above zero are exactly those labels. | `services/minutes_division.py`: `_read_list`, `_get_side_to_start`. | A shortfall may mean names crossed sides; indistinguishable columns cannot place names. | Not measured; the first supervised dry run of `apply_minutes_divisions` logs it. | A Hansard name list that cannot be read, or whose side counts differ from the printed counts, gets no vote rows for that division. | The columns of a Hansard name list printed in a `<pre>` block are the strips that stay blank on every name line of the block, with a tab counted as 8 positions. A column with text on fewer than 2 name lines (5 lines where the gap before it is 1 position wide) is no column, and its text stays in the column to its left. A line holding a heading word is not a name line, and each cell on it takes the column its offset falls in. No STORED list changes. A name printed under the wrong heading in a column the strips did read would pass if the counts matched. | Yes, using `report.content`. | Maintainers (votes and leave build, refused vote lists) |
| A heading that stands alone on a row of a Hansard `<pre>` block, more than 6 positions from every column start, applies to every column of the block, as "Absent" printed between the Ayes and Noes columns (24 May 1967) heads both columns of names under it. | `services/division_list.py`: `_get_spanning_heading_cells`. | The record prints one heading over a block of two columns, in the gap between them. | Counted on 19 November 2026 by the dry run of `apply_divisions`, against the plan of the Nils cell: no division changes status or any field. ## Register: everything outside speech text

These steps change data outside speech text. Counts were taken on 17 October 2026 on `hansard_replica` unless stated otherwise; Figures no query can repeat name their date and source. "Not measured" means no document and no light query gave a count. Name cleaning and resolution use sitting markdown, the structured attendance list, and the `Speaker` table, so the raw layer can rebuild every row. Stored `speaker_name` is the exception: stage 7 stores the resolved name without the printed form (see the cascade row below). The rows for attendance names after the DS-06, DS-10 and write #6 changes are in the main register above.

### Reports and raw rows

| What changes or is dropped | Where in code | Why | Cost | Reversible from the raw tables | Decided |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Only the first raw row for a `report_id` becomes a `Report`. Later copies stay in `hansardwebsiteresponse` without a report, speeches, or links. Search can repeat a hit; later copies are not compared. Repeated hits within a run are also dropped before fetching, keeping the first. | `populate/reports.py`: `populate_reports`; `populate/hansard_responses.py`: `populate_hansard_responses` (both keep the first by `setdefault`) | Repeats duplicate the `Report` and all its speech rows. | 47,402 raw rows, 41,811 distinct `report_id`, so 5,591 later copies carry no report. 41,801 reports: the other 10 are the excluded reports of the DS-04 row above. Each of the 5,591 ids has exactly 2 raw rows matching in content, title, subtitle, type, sitting date, file name, and version. The pair differs only in `speech_number` (`sno`), so the later copy's number is lost. | Yes. Every copy remains in the raw table. | No recorded decision (`28259c7`) |
| Fetched report bodies replace NUL bytes with U+FFFD before storage. The gateway strips NUL from sitting bodies, so the two raw tables treat NUL differently. | `services/hansard_website.py`: `get_topic_html_content`, `_NUL_REPLACEMENT` | Postgres text cannot hold NUL. | Not measured. NUL becomes U+FFFD before storage, and SPRS also sends U+FFFD, so only refetching these bodies can identify former NUL characters. The run of 26 October 2026 refetched sittings only, and the maintainers ruled out any other SPRS crawl that day. Upper bound (query C1a, counted on 26 October 2026 on the replica): 251 U+FFFD characters in 87 raw bodies, all older-system, of 83 reports (four reports have two raw rows). From the surrounding characters, 158 follow a digit and stand for a lost "½" or another character in transcripts of 1955 to 1998 ("7� hours"); 31 stand before a "?", mostly a lost closing quote; the rest replace a dash, a pound sign or an accented letter. | No for NUL positions: replacement precedes storage. The source API still serves it. See "Exceptions to raw first". | No recorded decision (`ee99806`) |
| Missing text fields and failed fetches store NULL content and log the hit. Empty strings stay empty strings. The fill stage skips newer-system `atbp` reports, which return no text, and retries only newer-system rows. | `populate/hansard_responses.py`: `_fetch_html_content`; `populate/hansard_response_contents.py`: `populate_hansard_response_contents` | A report with no text cannot be parsed. | 140 raw rows hold NULL content (114 distinct ids, 130 of the rows of type `atbp`) and 17 hold an empty string. 114 reports have NULL content. | Yes. A later fetch fills it in place. | No recorded decision (`652ffe3`) |
| 18 stored non-`atbp` reports lack text, markdown, and speech rows: 8 newer-system reports with NULL content and 10 older-system reports with an empty string. All 18 are source failures, and no one chose to drop this text. Report 20612, the 19th, was also empty, but SPRS served its text; `scripts/apply_coverage_refill.py` restored it with 75 speech rows. The list is Appendix A. | `populate/hansard_responses.py`: `_fetch_html_content`; `populate/hansard_response_contents.py`: `populate_hansard_response_contents` (retries newer-system rows only, so an older-system empty string is never retried) | One request per report through `get_hansard_topic_response`, counted on 17 Oct 2026, found: the 8 newer-system ids answer with an error body and no `resultHTML`; 10 of the 11 older-system file names answer with an empty `html_content`; 20612 answers with 48,228 characters. The raw rows also lack text. Reports 7423 and 11663 are old ids whose items are stored with text under `clarification-1902#` (report 47402) and `budget-1318#` (report 47401). | 18 reports, 0 speech rows, text size unknown because the source holds none. 20612 lacked 48,228 characters and 75 speech rows until its refill. The earlier count named only 2 of the 19 ids (`vision.md`); 17 were unnamed. | 20612: rebuilt. The other 18: no source text to rebuild. | Maintainers (third audit, done test O1) |
| Raw search-hit `title` and `subtitle` pass to `Report` through `fix_mojibake`, which re-reads each non-ASCII run as UTF-8 when possible. Runs failing either decode stay unchanged. `scripts/apply_report_titles.py` applies the fix to stored titles too. | `utils/text.py`: `fix_mojibake`; `services/report.py`: `build_report`; `scripts/apply_report_titles.py` | Titles from the older system arrive as mojibake. | 0 stored `original_title` values differ from the raw title. `apply_report_titles.py` rewrote 11 stored titles on production: 7 budget titles from "âˆ’" to "−" and 4 President's Address titles from "â€™" to "’". No subtitle changed. No title or subtitle holds "â", "Ã" or "Â" after it. The 11 `original_title` values keep the source's mojibake. The new rule changes 1 of 52,250 raw titles that the old rule left alone (report 20433) and moves no speech start line in the 21,845 older-system reports. | Yes. The raw title remains. | Maintainers (`a42cc84`) |
| Reports changed by SPRS after the daily refresh keep their first text; the run alerts without rewriting. | `populate/refresh_runs.py`: `_get_changed_reports` | The refresh adds rows only (launch brief decisions 21, 22). | `refresh_run.reports_changed` per run. 0 in runs 4 and 5, counted on 19 October 2026; run 5 re-checked 300 reports. | No. Raw rows keep only the first text; a supervised fill can store the replacement. | Maintainers |
| Textless search hits are not stored. Each run retries them while their sitting is within 14 days, then drops them with an alert. | `populate/refresh_runs.py`: `_write_window_rows` | Filling a raw row later requires an update forbidden by the brief. | `refresh_run.reports_held_without_text`. | No raw row exists; supervised filling can fetch it. | Maintainers (launch build plan, brief decisions 21 and 25) |
| `report_put_to` reads the office from the first "asked the" or "To ask the" of each question row, when an office head follows, through the title's end. Where that opening is misprinted (no "the", "the the", "the,", "The", "he", a repeated verb or a bracket before "the") the first "ask" or "asked" of the row is read the same way, with source MISPRINTED_OPENING; a misprinted whole title on a closed list stays as printed. The table omits later text, non-question rows, and phrases without a listed head. A leading asterisk stays in `printed_text`, outside `office_printed`. | services/report_put_to.py: get_put_to_phrases | The table records who received a question; speech rows keep the full text. | Counted on 9 November 2026 on hansard_positions: 31,779 of 31,970 question reports give a phrase, 191 give none. Figures after the MISPRINTED_OPENING rule are in the phase 3 handoff. | Yes, from the speech rows. | Maintainers (plan step 7, gate 2) |
| `office_title_ministry` groups exact printed titles under a closed ministry-name list, using hand-kept entries for unreadable titles. Matching alone removes brackets, collapses spaces, and drops a leading "The". A role with no portfolio gets no ministry. Any unresolved clause prevents a title row. | services/office_title_ministry.py: get_office_title_placement; data/ministry_names.json; data/office_title_ministries.json | A ministry page lists the titles printed under it. A row claims no ministry of that name existed. | Counted on 9 November 2026: 1,196 titles, 1,101 placed to a ministry (976 by the rule alone, 125 using a hand-kept entry), 62 role-only, 33 left unplaced. The hand-kept list holds 129 entries. | Yes, using printed titles and both lists. | Maintainers (G6a, G6b, G6c, G7, gate 2) |

### Sittings

| What changes or is dropped | Where in code | Why | Cost | Reversible from the raw tables | Decided |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Every empty string in a sitting row becomes NULL. | `services/sitting.py`: `build_sitting` | Some `Optional[int]` columns are stored as VARCHAR, so the dump returns "" and Pydantic cannot coerce it to int. The rule covers all columns, including non-integers. | 386 raw rows hold "" in `online_pdf_file_name`, and no other column holds an empty string. All 1,719 sittings store NULL there. | Yes. Raw sitting rows keep "". | No recorded decision (`124922c`) |
| In an older-format sitting response, null or empty session, volume, parliament, or sitting numbers become NULL. Non-integers raise `HansardSittingReportError`, stopping the stage without storage. Numeric strings become integers. | `services/hansard_sitting_date_response.py`: `_get_int`, `build_old_hansard_sitting_date_response` | The raw columns are integers and the API sends them as strings or objects. This conflicts with "No type casting" in `CLAUDE.md` (Database models); the raw table is not as received for these four columns. A value that is not a number is a fault in the body, so a supervised backfill stops on it rather than store a NULL. | Not measured as a count of values this rule changed: before 23 Oct 2026 a value that is not an integer became NULL, and the stored rows cannot show whether a NULL came from null, an empty string or such a value, because the text as received is not kept. What the rows hold (query C2a, counted on 26 October 2026 on the replica): all 1,333 older-format sitting rows have a NULL parliament number and a NULL session number, and 204 a NULL sitting number (every older sitting from 2004 to 2010, and 17 in 2003 and 2011). The pattern (every older-format row; every sitting from 2004 to 2010) points to the response sending no value, not to values this rule turned into NULL. The stored masthead prints the parliament number in all 1,333, the session number in 1,332 and the sitting number in all 1,333 (query C2c), and `sitting.sitting_no` was filled from it for all 204. | Yes for null and an empty string, which carry no number. In the stored lists (query C12a) 1 entry has no `mpName` (sitting 7385) and 11 are the leave notes of the row above. ### Stage 8: speaker links

| What changes or is dropped | Where in code | Why | Cost | Reversible from the raw tables | Decided |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A speech row gets a `speaker_id` only if its name resolves; its stored `speaker` string stays unchanged. Lookup removes a list number, a leading dot, trailing "Mr Speaker", rank qualifier "(NS)" or "(Res)", trailing `;`, `,`, or `:`, an unclosed bracket, a doubled label, and a leading title dot. "Surname, Given" becomes "Given Surname"; "Assoc.Prof." gains a space. A title in brackets names the person; other brackets are cut. The row above covers DS-09 cleaning. Attendance links use stored names without this cleaning. | `services/speaker_link.py`: `_clean_speaker_label`, `resolve_speech_speaker_id`, `_resolve_attendance_speaker_id` | Labels carry office text, list numbers, and OCR noise around names. | 951,956 speech rows have a speaker string; 17,371 (1.82%) lack `speaker_id`. Hand checking 613 linked rows found all 613 names mapped to the right person; 98.4% held that person's speech (counted on 16 Oct 2026, sample not repeated). | Yes. The speaker string stays unchanged; links can be recomputed. | No recorded decision (`4274472`) |
| Rows get no link if their speaker is "An hon. Member", "Some hon. Members", "Non-Residents", "Tributes by Leader of the House and Opposition Leaders", or starts with "(" or "_". | `utils/speaker_resolution_constants.py`: `NON_SPEAKERS`; `services/speaker_link.py`: `resolve_speech_speaker_id` | These labels name no one. | Query C4a, counted on 26 October 2026 on the replica: 2,003 speech rows have skipped labels; none is linked. "An hon. Member": 1,663 (712 reports); "Some hon. Members": 304 (231 reports); labels starting with "(": 3; with "_": 33. "Non-Residents" and the Tributes label have no rows. Of the 33 underscore rows, 31 hold a heading (report 25158) and 2 a named member with a stray underscore (`_S_ hawn Huang Wei Zhong (Jurong)`, report 7496). Of the 3 bracket rows, 2 have no text and 1 is a Deputy Speaker's adjournment motion. | Yes. | No recorded decision (`4dd036e`) |

With two holders, bare "Mr Deputy Speaker" stays unlinked unless the report names one or the sitting-level rule below credits it. Head-of-state strings link only in the Address. ### Bills, Acts and Legislative History

`scripts/apply_act_links.py` stores grouped bills, Acts harvested from Singapore Statutes Online (SSO), and their Legislative History entries. Bills come from `bill` and `bill-intro` reports, other report types that read bills, and reports listed in `data/hand_kept_bills.json`. Grouping counts use `hansard_replica`, counted on 4 November 2026. Entry counts use the dry run on 523 Acts and 9,905 entries, counted on 5 November 2026, unless a row gives a later date. Production holds 526 Acts from copies fetched on 13 November 2026, with 10,258 entries, including 1,902 across a page break.

| What changes or is dropped | Where in code | Why | Cost | Reversible from the raw tables | Decided |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Only an Act's SSO code, title, and Legislative History entries are stored, never its text. | `populate/act_links.py`: `write_act_link_plan` | Hosting the text of current law needs permission AGC can withdraw (`docs/report/bill-debate-linking-2026-11-04.md`). | The whole consolidated text of every Act. | No: SSO holds the text outside the corpus. | Maintainers (ruling 5) |
| 33 `bill` and `bill-intro` reports whose title has no word "bill" form no bill or `billreport` row: ADJOURNMENT, EXEMPTED BUSINESS and similar, 21 distinct titles. Two that do name bills in other words ("BILLS INTRODUCED", "SUPPLY AND SUPPLEMENTARY SUPPLY BILLS") are among them, so the first readings they hold link to no bill. | `services/bill.py`: `group_bills` | The title names no bill for grouping. | 33 of 5,839 reports counted on 4 November 2026. The plan lists each. | Yes: reports remain in `report` and `speech`. | No recorded decision |

Each run logs every report it leaves out as `Bill report <id> left out: <reason>`. | Yes. The report stays in `report` and `speech`, and the next `apply_act_links --commit` groups it, except a title with no word "bill", which the row above covers. | Maintainers (law links gate 1 ruling, question 1) |

| Reports are grouped by the title's lower case letters and digits, a bill closes on a report holding a third-reading line, a report holding a first-reading line on a later sitting date than the open bill's last report starts a new bill (a bill that lapsed at prorogation or dissolution and was read a first time again, or was withdrawn and replaced under the same title), and a gap of more than 366 days starts a new bill. A "Brought up, and read the First time" line, a new clause in committee, is no first reading. Punctuation, spacing, and case in a title are lost from the key; the stored `bill.title` is the last report's. A bill whose title changes between readings (1960, Hospitals Board (Repeal) Bill to Government Hospitals Bill) becomes two bills, except where `data/bill_title_corrections.json` corrects a misprinted title or `data/hand_kept_bills.json` joins the renamed bill (rows below). | `services/bill.py`: `get_title_key`, `group_bills` | The rule matched hand-built links for 72 of 72 sampled bills. | 2,811 bills, 2,682 of them passed, from 5,827 reports (2,820 bills and 5,806 reports before the title corrections, hand-kept bills and other-type readings); the first-reading rule added 52 of them by splitting 51. | Yes, from `report.title` and `speech.transcript`. | No recorded decision |

The stored `title` uses the correction when that report is last in its bill; `report.title` is never written. A bill takes exactly the listed reports, the given title and its title key, the first and last sitting dates of its reports, and is passed when one of them holds a third-reading line. The link is by title key (the title less a leading "The", a trailing "(Chapter N)" and its year, "Act" or "Ordinance" turned to "Bill", "Bill" added after any other last word; a title printed with a leading "The" is also tried with the "The" kept, first, because two bills keep it) and a report within one day of the first, then the third, then the second reading date. An entry with no exact match then tries a looser key (the title key with "amendment", "amendment" with a number and a standalone number removed) and links only when exactly one bill with that key has a report within a day of the first reading date (the third when no first is printed) and, where a third is printed, of that date too. A misprint in the Act's title or the bill's ("Feeding Stuffs" for "FEEDSTUFFS", "AMANDMENT") links only through a hand-kept entry (next row); no similarity score makes a link (hansard ADR 0011). `scripts/apply_report_links.py` stores `report_link` rows from citations printed in speech rows.

| What changes or is dropped | Where in code | Why | Cost | Reversible from the raw tables | Decided |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| Only the comparison collapses whitespace in citation titles and `report.original_title`. ### API-side cleaning (the web app, `roycehoe/hansard`)

The API changes only responses: it reads tables through a read-only role and writes nothing. Removing the code reverses each change below. Paths are in `roycehoe/hansard`. The party row above covers the merge of the two People's Action Party spellings.

| What changes or is dropped | Where in code | Why | Cost | Reversible from the raw tables | Decided |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A report has text only if a speech row points at it. A report with a markdown header but no speeches has no text on its report page, sitting and search lists, text filter, and coverage counts. | `api/crud/report.py`: `_get_is_text_available` | Testing only non-empty markdown showed "has text" over an empty speech list. | 654 reports on `hansard_w4` at the commit, counted on 16 Oct 2026 (not repeated). On `hansard_replica` today 126 reports have no speech row, and 125 of them have no text either, so 1 report with markdown reads as having none. | Yes. | No recorded decision (`d3dc4de`) |
| Search snippets and headlines lose blockquote, bullet, and heading markers, link targets, underscore emphasis, and every asterisk. Snippets end at 35 words with an ellipsis. Whitespace collapses. | `api/utils/markdown_text.py`: `strip_markdown`; `api/utils/transcript_snippet.py`: `get_transcript_snippet`; `api/services/search.py` | Result lists showed raw markdown (commits `aa78dfc`, `33a70ed`, `a6f4439`). | Replica, counted on 26 October 2026 over all 1,115,831 transcripts, with the API's `strip_markdown` in a Python pass and with query C6b: 32,217 change by more than whitespace, 26,841 of them for a reason other than an asterisk (mostly underscore emphasis, in 26,431). 5,376 hold an asterisk; in 677 it sits inside a word, and the name loses it (`A*STAR` becomes "ASTAR", `S*Bio` becomes "SBio"). 1,677 hold a bullet marker, 39 a heading marker, 24 a blockquote marker and 7 a link. 32 transcripts with text become empty (`####`). 730,018 are over 35 words after stripping, so the snippet on the path with no search words cuts them. With search words the snippet comes from `ts_headline` and is stripped after, so these counts are an upper bound there. | Yes. The transcript is unchanged. | No recorded decision (`aa78dfc`, `33a70ed`, `a6f4439`) |
| Copied speech citations take names from speaker labels rather than `person_name`, and offices from label text before the name bracket. `person_name` is used only when the label is an office with no name bracket, and a stored "Surname, Given" form is ignored. | `app/src/lib/citation.ts`: `parseSpeakerLabel`, `buildSpeechCitation` | `person_name` dropped the honorific ("Prof S Jayakumar" became "S Jayakumar"). | 2,297 of 962,541 citations on `hansard_w4` differ from the code before `a621af5` (dry run counted on 16 Oct 2026, not repeated; the code has changed since). | Yes. | No recorded decision (`a621af5`, `7063311`) |
| A `SPEECH` row without a speaker shows a muted "Speaker not recorded" label instead of nothing. Absent labels also become empty response strings. | `app/src/components/reports/RowSpeech.tsx`: `renderSpeakerLabel`; `api/services/report.py`, `api/services/search.py` (`speech.speaker or ""`) | The missing label suggested the previous speaker's words. | 488 rows in 10 reports (`hansard_replica`, counted on 17 Oct 2026). | Yes. | No recorded decision (`78534d2`) |
| The sitting attendance list shows `person.canonical_name` and sorts by it (present first, then the shown name casefolded), so the stored key form ("Chen Tien Lap, Bernard", "Chandra Das, S.") no longer appears on that page. Rows without a Person use the stored name. | `app/src/components/sittings/ListAttendance.tsx`; `api/services/sitting.py`: `_get_attendance_sort_key` | Lookup key names read badly in lists (DS2-07). | 14,266 rows in 1,281 sittings show a different name; 3 rows have no Person and show the stored name. | Yes: remove the code; stored rows are unchanged. | Maintainers (DS2-07, persona pass 2; `d05883e`) |
| Listed takedown reports return HTTP 410 on their pages and `/api/reports/{slug}`, with no preview tags. They are excluded from report search and browse results, speech search results and the search totals, their sittings' report lists, and person report lists. A party change sourced to a hidden report shows no source report on the person page and the people list. Counts are unchanged: the coverage and Parliament figures, a sitting's report count and person speech counts. | `api/takedown-list.txt`; `api/utils/report_slug.py`: `get_normalised_report_slug`; `api/utils/takedown_list.py`; `api/middleware/takedown.py`: `is_report_slug_taken_down`, `is_report_id_taken_down`, `get_taken_down_report_id_candidates`, `TakedownMiddleware`; `api/website.py`; `api/crud/speech.py`, `api/crud/report.py` (`excluded_report_ids`); `api/services/report.py`, `api/services/search.py`, `api/services/sitting.py`; `api/services/person.py`: `_get_membership_id_to_party_affiliations`, `_get_source_report_data` | Parliament may request removal; the letter promises it within 2 working days (launch build brief, scenario 3). | 0 reports at the time of the commit; one per line of the list after that. | Yes: delete the line; stored rows are unchanged. | Maintainers (launch build brief, scenario 3; `5624c93`, `55ada61`, `d1d3646`, `82d7e54`, `0e5151a`, `5bdca60`, `0d335fa`, `bf71e55`, `d92c02c`) |
| The seat filter (`constituency` on `GET /search/speeches`, the "Speeches by members for X" page) leaves out speeches whose speaker label is a chair title: Mr or Mdm Speaker, Deputy Speaker, The Chairman, Deputy or temporary Chairman, with or without a name in brackets, or a name followed by "(Mr Speaker)" or "(Deputy Speaker)". Those lines are credited to the presiding member's Membership, so a Speaker's seat opened on procedure ("Go ahead."). Person filters, report pages, and plain word searches still show them. Two stray labels are not caught ("Nr Speaker", "Dr Lee Boon Yang Mr Speaker,", 1 row each). | `api/services/search.py`: `_CHAIR_SPEAKER_LABEL_PATTERN`, `_get_excluded_speaker_pattern`; applied by `_get_excluded_speaker_filters` in `api/crud/speech.py` | R27 audit finding 2: a seat view promises what its members said. The lasting fix is scraper classification of chair lines as `PROCEDURAL`. | 33,062 of 987,180 speeches inside a seat span leave seat results (`hansard_launch` copy, counted on 24 Oct 2026). Marine Parade 10,838 of 48,177, East Coast 4,907 of 30,004, Brickworks 834 of 4,070. | Yes: remove the filter; stored rows are unchanged. | Maintainers (R27 medium findings, decision 10; `16c9a75`) |

## Limits of the record

Five limits affect how the derived data can be used.

- Column numbers ("Column: 293" markers) are removed from the markdown, and `speech.column_start` keeps the column each older-system row starts in (the register row on column numbers). 29,027 rows in reports with markers have none, because the markers do not fix it. Parliament 12 and early Parliament 13 print page numbers, which are not stored (R20 decision 3), and the newer system prints neither.
- `speech.id` and (report, ordinal) are not stable across writes. Write #2 deleted 24,402 rows, write #3 shifted about 600,000 ordinals, and write #4 deletes 443 rows, inserts 66, and updates 3,234 ordinals. Only `pg_dump` files outside git hold the old ids. A citation to a row id is valid for one database state only.
- The rebuild of 1,568 near-match stale reports rested on a sample of 30 read against the source, none of which showed a hand edit. A sample of 30 with no hits bounds the hand-edit rate at under 9.5% (95% confidence); it does not show the rate is zero. Write #3 shipped and the pre-write dump is outside git, so the check cannot be repeated.
- Stage 7 writes attendance once per sitting: `populate_attendances` skips every sitting that already has rows, so a later change to name cleaning reaches no stored sitting until its rows are deleted or a script rewrites them. This loses nothing; it is why the attendance rows above each need a targeted write.
- The ADR 0002 misprint rate is unmeasured: 5 "Mdm Speaker" rows are known. The Knesset and GermaParl figures quoted in `vision.md` are point estimates on other units, not like-for-like rates.

## Exceptions to raw first

Four exceptions mean the raw layer does not always hold the response as received.

- A NUL byte in a fetched report body becomes U+FFFD, not a deletion (`services/hansard_website.py`). The row is described under "Reports and raw rows".
- The gateway strips NUL bytes from a sitting response before the schema is built, so the stored row is what the schema saw (`utils/text.py::strip_nul`, `STYLE.md` sweep ruling 14, commit `d557ce7`). Count: not measured.
- Newer-system reports store only `resultHTML.content`. The other `resultHTML` fields, such as `mpNames` and `footNote`, have no column and are not stored. Footnote text is absent from the record for those reports; the footnote markers stay in the HTML ([ADR 0003](adr/0003-newer-system-raw-mapping.md)). Count (query C15a, counted on 26 October 2026 on the replica): 1,004 of the 24,803 newer-system reports hold a footnote marker in the form the parser reads, 2,042 markers in all. A wider scan, read by eye, finds 29 more reports with a marker that holds `&nbsp;` or a zero-width character inside the tag, which that form misses. None of the 24,803 holds a footnote section (`<div class="fns">`), and none of the 34 that use the word "footnote" holds a note's text.
- The newer-system sitting fields `sections`, `annexures`, `vernaculars`, and `a2b` are stored on both `sitting` and the raw `hansardsittingdateresponse` table ([ADR 0003](adr/0003-newer-system-raw-mapping.md), item 3). A rebuild of either table starts from the other. For the 425 newer-format sittings stored before 13 November 2026, `scripts/copy_sitting_lists_to_raw.py` copied the lists from `sitting` with SQL and made no request to SPRS, so the raw copy is the list as of the first fetch (or the fill stage on 14 October 2026), not a fresh read. A list SPRS changed after that is in neither table; that comparison was not made (the 26 October refetch compared `attendance_list` only). An older-format sitting has all four null on both tables. The daily refresh and the fill stage (`populate/sitting_details.py`) write both tables from the response they fetched. Size: about 250 MB of text, nearly all in `sections`. Decided: Maintainers [ldg-raw-lists-copy]: Do.

## A metric change, not a data change

The text-share denominator excludes the `Title:` and `MPs Speaking:` header fields. The earlier denominator counted the `Title:` and `MPs Speaking:` header fields as body text the parser dropped. They were 1.72M of 188.1M characters in Parliaments 0 to 11. Commit `7ba038f` leaves them out. On the same stored rows, text share moves from 96.59% to 97.48%. F1, the removal of report headers from single-speaker rows, lowered it by 0.07 points, because 135,406 characters of metadata had counted as linked speech. Text dropped inside reports that have rows fell from 1.22% to 0.41%. No stored text changed for this row. The figures before and after it are not comparable without the note.

Counting preambles as rows with no speaker moves 505,925 characters from dropped to rows with no speaker. A second change (commit `efa477d`) leaves the `(proc text)` tag out of the body character count in `scripts/attribution_text_share.py`, because it is markup: about 221,000 (22 per tag, 10,060 tags) characters. Neither moves stored text.

## Per-report exceptions

### Applied after hand review

| Report | What was read | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 43315 | The F7 shape `****Mr X** :**` put a minister's answers under Mr Speaker and Mdm Halimah. | Applied in the combined write. 17 rows moved from Mr Speaker to Mr Lim Hng Kiang and 1 row was restored. |
| 6643 | F2: one stored row had swallowed about 10,500 words. | Applied in the combined write. The report went from 210 rows to 431, with 221 rows inserted, and its speaker sequence now matches the source's labels. |

### Held on purpose

| Report | Why |
|---|---|
| 22647, 22650 | Hand-edited earlier. The combined plan marks both `hold:hand_edited_drift`. They stay as stored. They also carry the F7 shape. |
| 23921 | Held from write #2. D1 recovers a lost answer but under Miss Penny Low, because of a broken label, so lost text would become a wrong speaker. Write #12 released it because the current parser reads the broken label whole and credits the recovered answer to Dr Mohamad Maliki Bin Osman. |
| 38225 | Held from write #2. WS2 fixes 4 wrong-speaker rows but deletes the fragment "1964, totalled 99, including rehearsals, etc. The", which was stored only as a label. It waits for a fix that moves the fragment into the speech. |
| 41944 | Held from write #2. F5 would credit the chamber event "...the **Chief Minister** rose -" to Mr Speaker. |
| 20991, 21557 | F2 split a vernacular note into two rows, a regression found by hand. Held among the 17 reports the checker could not explain. |

Other holds from the round 1 combined write, by plan status: 111 reports whose stored markdown no longer matches the parser (`hold:drift`), 584 stale reports (`hold:stale`), 17 with changes the checker could not explain (`hold:unexplained`), and the 2 hand-edited reports above. That is 714 reports held after the write. The stale re-parse rebuilds only reports whose stored rows exactly equal some parser's output from their stored markdown, which proves no hand edit. Reports with ordinal gaps, deleted rows, or rows no parser produces stay held and go on a list for the maintainers.

The write #3 planner records the applied and held reports: plan v2, before changes A to E, applied 11,271 reports and held 162. The final plan, v4, holds 162 reports, each listed with its reason in the plan. A report write #3 does not rebuild keeps its stored rows. Its kind is set only where a stored row equals a parsed row, and stays NULL elsewhere (4,146 rows in 162 reports).

Write #4 rebuilds 42 of those 162 reports, the 22149 class and 38719 (the register row above gives the evidence). A read of the 47 held reports whose stored rows credit text to a string that names no person explained 41 of them, and those are released. The other 6 stay held: 22806 (a caption and a bold table cell are still read as labels), 23268 (a footnote run swallows the speech after it), 23627 (the parser drops a "Table 1:" caption), 23038 and 24839 (stored rows are missing, with no recorded cause), and 41804 (held by decision). 121 reports stay held after write #4 (the 120 left of write #3's 162, and 24248, which gets no write), and kind stays NULL on 2,275 rows, down from 4,146. 38719, a held report outside the 47, is released with the 41 after a separate read. Write #9 released 22806, 23627, 23038, and 24839 with 56 other held reports (the write #9 row); 23268 and 41804 stay held.

Round 0 held back, with overlaps: 121 stale reports, 16 reports whose stored markdown still holds `**Column : 293**` lines (ids 37916 to 39514), 10 reports where spacing the entity exposes html2text escapes, and 6 whose row count would change. Reports 23398 and 23401 would lose a footnote, 22602 would gain a junk row, and 22073, 22636, and 24516 would gain rows.

### Tier A reports listed instead of fixed

Tier A reports cannot be fixed safely by a parser rule, so they are listed (stopping rule, "Residue instead of a fix"). Counts and ordinals describe stored rows counted on 12 November 2026 (the 15 October figures differed for 17279 and for 42017, which later writes renumbered or re-parsed). No text is dropped. The reports are listed individually, with no override file. Write #3 sets their `speaker` and `speaker_id` to NULL, so no row names a wrong member. The kind is SPEECH, except for the rows of 42017, which are UNCLASSIFIED. The maintainers ruled that stage 8 stores the right speaker as a `speaker_id`, with no label, from `data/unlabelled_speech.json`; 42017 stays out.

Write #2 deleted rows without renumbering, so the ranges for 17279 and 18272 hold fewer rows than their ordinals span, and two CHAMBER_EVENT rows inside the ranges (280 ordinal 363, 16500 ordinal 156) stay uncredited.

Report 24248 joins the list. It is a debate on the President's Address, and the mover's opening speech, about 14,000 characters by Mr Gan Kim Yong, has its label merged into the heading. The stored rows do not hold the speech. Write #3 adds it as rows with no speaker and kind SPEECH (30 rows, named in `data/unlabelled_speech.json`), so no stored text is lost. The record prints no label for it, so the rows keep none; the file credits them to Mr Gan Kim Yong, Speaker 725 in Parliament 10, and stage 8 stores that as a `speaker_id`. With these 30 rows the credit covers 488 rows in 10 reports.

The eight reports where the chair's line is followed by an unlabelled resumption share one shape, but no rule is safe for it. After a short "Yes ..." the chair really does keep talking in reports 35601, 38974, 38527, and 41633, so a rule that hands the lines back to the member who asked would misattribute those.

### B. Reports that lose more than 5% of their HTML word tokens (19)

Method. A token is a run of ASCII letters and digits, lower-cased. Per report, the share is the HTML tokens that the markdown does not hold, as a multiset difference, over all HTML tokens. HTML comments, scripts, tags, `Column: N` and `Page: N` markers, and `(proc text)` are removed from the HTML first, because earlier rows record those removals. The figures equal the third audit's list report for report. All 41,801 reports were counted. Each report was read against its markdown, and the 16 head-title reports were counted a second time without the HTML head, which gave 0 tokens lost.

## Queries for the measurements of 26 October 2026

Each query ran read only on `hansard_replica` (`PGOPTIONS='-c default_transaction_read_only=on'`). The sweep ran through the night of 26 to 27 October 2026, and each figure carries the date the sweep started. Except where a cell says otherwise, a second method (a different query or a separate Python pass) confirmed each figure before it entered the register. The queries are as they ran, except C2c, which is the second method's shape, and C9a, which writes the byte order mark as `\uFEFF` instead of the literal character.

### C1a: U+FFFD in raw report bodies (NUL row)

```sql
select report_version, count(*) as bodies, sum(regexp_count(content, U&'\FFFD')) as characters
from hansardwebsiteresponse
where strpos(content, U&'\FFFD') > 0
group by report_version order by report_version;

select 'raw' as side, count(*) as rows, count(distinct report_id) as distinct_report_ids
from hansardwebsiteresponse where strpos(content, U&'\FFFD') > 0
union all
select 'report', count(*), count(distinct report_id) from report where strpos(content, U&'\FFFD') > 0;
```

### C2a: NULL numbers in older-format raw sitting rows

```sql
select count(*) as older_rows,
  count(*) filter (where parlement_no is null) as parliament_null,
  count(*) filter (where session_no is null) as session_null,
  count(*) filter (where volume_no is null) as volume_null,
  count(*) filter (where sitting_no is null) as sitting_null,
  count(*) filter (where parlement_no is null or session_no is null or volume_no is null or sitting_no is null) as any_null
from hansardsittingdateresponse
where report_version is not null or html_full_content is not null;
```

### C2c: numbers the older-format masthead prints

```sql
select count(*) as older_rows,
  count(*) filter (where html_full_content ~ 'Parliament No:(\s|<[^>]*>|&nbsp;)*[0-9]+') as parliament_printed,
  count(*) filter (where html_full_content ~ 'Session No:(\s|<[^>]*>|&nbsp;)*[0-9]+') as session_printed,
  count(*) filter (where html_full_content ~ 'Sitting No:(\s|<[^>]*>|&nbsp;)*[0-9]+') as sitting_printed
from hansardsittingdateresponse
where html_full_content is not null;
```

### C3a: report dates with no sitting row, raw or derived

```sql
with report_days as (
  select sitting_date::date as day, min(report_version) as report_version, count(*) as reports
  from report group by 1
), sitting_days as (
  select distinct to_date(sitting_date, 'DD-MM-YYYY') as day from sitting
), raw_days as (
  select distinct to_date(sitting_date, 'DD-MM-YYYY') as day from hansardsittingdateresponse
)
select r.report_version, count(*) as days, sum(r.reports) as reports, min(r.day), max(r.day)
from report_days r
where not exists (select 1 from sitting_days s where s.day = r.day)
  and not exists (select 1 from raw_days w where w.day = r.day)
group by r.report_version order by 1;
```

### C4a: labels the non-speaker rule leaves unlinked

```sql
select case
         when speaker in ('An hon. Member', 'Some hon. Members', 'Non-Residents',
                          'Tributes by Leader of the House and Opposition Leaders') then speaker
         when speaker like '(%' then 'starts with ('
         when speaker like '\_%' then 'starts with _'
       end as label_class,
       count(*) as rows, count(distinct report_id) as reports,
       count(*) filter (where speaker_id is not null) as linked
from speech
where speaker in ('An hon. Member', 'Some hon. Members', 'Non-Residents',
                  'Tributes by Leader of the House and Opposition Leaders')
   or speaker like '(%' or speaker like '\_%'
group by 1 order by 1;
```

### C5a: chair labels and their links

The split by label in the cell is the same query grouped by `speaker`; the 1,442 reports are counted over all three chairman labels together.

```sql
select case
         when speaker ~* '^(the ?|mr ?)?chairman:?$' then 'Chairman'
         when btrim(speaker) ~* '^mr deputy speaker:?$' then 'bare Mr Deputy Speaker'
         when speaker = 'The Chief Secretary' then 'The Chief Secretary'
       end as label_class,
       count(*) as rows,
       count(*) filter (where speaker_id is not null) as linked,
       count(distinct report_id) filter (where speaker_id is not null) as reports_with_link
from speech
where speaker ~* '^(the ?|mr ?)?chairman:?$'
   or btrim(speaker) ~* '^mr deputy speaker:?$'
   or speaker = 'The Chief Secretary'
group by 1 order by 1;
```

### C6b: transcripts holding each markdown class the search snippet strips

```sql
select count(*) as rows,
  count(*) filter (where strpos(transcript, '*') > 0) as asterisk,
  count(*) filter (where transcript ~ '(^|\n)[ \t]*#{1,6}([[:space:]]|$)') as heading,
  count(*) filter (where transcript ~ '(^|\n)[ \t]*>') as blockquote,
  count(*) filter (where transcript ~ '(^|\n)[ \t]*[-*+][ \t]+') as bullet,
  count(*) filter (where transcript ~ '!?\[[^]]*\]\([^)[:space:]]*\)') as link,
  count(*) filter (where transcript ~ '(^|[^[:alnum:]_])_[^_[:space:]]') as underscore_open,
  count(*) filter (where array_length(regexp_split_to_array(btrim(transcript), '[[:space:]]+'), 1) > 35) as over_35_words
from speech;
```

### C7a and C7b: column numbers in older-system reports with markers

```sql
select count(*) as rows, count(distinct s.report_id) as reports,
  count(s.column_start) as with_column, count(*) - count(s.column_start) as without_column,
  count(*) filter (where s.column_note = 'LIKELY_MISPRINT') as likely_misprint,
  count(distinct s.report_id) filter (where s.column_note = 'LIKELY_MISPRINT') as likely_misprint_reports,
  count(*) filter (where s.column_note = 'SPEAKER_NAMED_IN_COLUMN_BEFORE') as speaker_named_before,
  count(*) filter (where s.column_note = 'PRINTED_WITH_L_FOR_1') as printed_l_for_1
from speech s join report r on r.id = s.report_id
where r.report_version = 'sprs2' and r.content ~ 'Column(\s+No)?\s*:\s*\d+';

select count(*) as with_markers, count(*) filter (where column_start is null) as no_range
from report where report_version = 'sprs2' and content ~ 'Column(\s+No)?\s*:\s*\d+';
```

### C8a: rows that still open with a stray close mark (language note row)

```sql
select count(*) as rows, count(distinct report_id) as reports
from speech
where transcript like '\_[\_%' or transcript like '\_ %';
```

### C9a: what the newer-system cleanup removes, in raw report text

```sql
select report_version, count(*) as reports,
  count(*) filter (where content ~ '<(strong|em)[^>]*>([[:space:]]|&nbsp;|\uFEFF)*</\1>') as with_empty_emphasis,
  sum(regexp_count(content, '<(strong|em)[^>]*>([[:space:]]|&nbsp;|\uFEFF)*</\1>')) as empty_emphasis_tags,
  count(*) filter (where strpos(content, '(proc text)') > 0) as with_proc_text,
  sum(regexp_count(content, '\(proc text\)')) as proc_text_markers,
  count(*) filter (where content ~ '<img[^>]*src="data:[^"]*"[^>]*>') as with_data_image,
  sum(regexp_count(content, '<img[^>]*src="data:[^"]*"[^>]*>')) as data_images,
  sum(length(content) - length(regexp_replace(content, '<img[^>]*src="data:[^"]*"[^>]*>', '', 'g'))) as data_image_characters,
  count(*) filter (where strpos(content, U&'\FEFF') > 0) as with_bom,
  sum(length(content) - length(replace(content, U&'\FEFF', ''))) as bom_characters
from report group by report_version order by 1;
```

### C10a, C10b and C10c: Column and Page markers

```sql
select report_version, count(*) as reports,
  count(*) filter (where content ~ 'Column(\s+No)?\s*:\s*\d+') as with_column_marker,
  sum(regexp_count(content, 'Column(\s+No)?\s*:\s*\d+')) as column_markers,
  count(*) filter (where content ~ 'Page:\s*\d+') as with_page_text,
  sum(regexp_count(content, 'Page:\s*\d+')) as page_texts
from report group by report_version order by 1;

select count(*) as sittings,
  count(*) filter (where html_full_content ~ 'Column(\s+No)?\s*:\s*\d+') as with_column_marker,
  sum(regexp_count(html_full_content, 'Column(\s+No)?\s*:\s*\d+')) as column_markers,
  count(*) filter (where html_full_content ~ 'Page:\s*\d+') as with_page_text,
  sum(regexp_count(html_full_content, 'Page:\s*\d+')) as page_texts
from sitting where html_full_content is not null;

select count(*) as stored_markdowns_with_marker, min(id) as min_id, max(id) as max_id
from report where markdown_content ~ 'Column(\s+No)?\s*:\s*\d+';
```

### C11a: stored rows that credit a heading or a bare time stamp to a speaker

```sql
select count(*) filter (where transcript ~ '^#{1,6}([[:space:]]|$)') as heading_rows,
       count(*) filter (where strpos(transcript, '####') > 0) as hash4_rows,
       count(*) filter (where transcript ~* '^[0-9]{1,2}([[:space:]]*[.:][[:space:]]*[0-9]{2})?[[:space:]]*([ap]\.?m|noon|midnight)\.?$') as time_rows
from speech where speaker is not null;
```

### C12a: attendance list entries with no name, and leave notes

```sql
select count(*) as entries,
  count(*) filter (where e->>'mpName' is null) as no_mp_name,
  count(*) filter (where e->>'mpName' like 'For information on permission given to Members for leave of absence%') as leave_notes,
  count(distinct s.id) filter (where e->>'mpName' like 'For information on permission given to Members for leave of absence%') as sittings_with_leave_note
from sitting s cross join lateral jsonb_array_elements(s.attendance_list::jsonb) e
where s.attendance_list is not null;
```

### C15a: newer-system footnote markers, with and without a footnote section

```sql
select count(*) as sprs3_reports,
  count(*) filter (where content ~ '<sup\M[^>]*>\s*(?:<a\M[^>]*>)?\s*\d{1,3}(?:\s*,\s*\d{1,3})*\s*(?:</a>)?\s*</sup>') as with_marker,
  sum(regexp_count(content, '<sup\M[^>]*>\s*(?:<a\M[^>]*>)?\s*\d{1,3}(?:\s*,\s*\d{1,3})*\s*(?:</a>)?\s*</sup>')) as markers,
  count(*) filter (where content ~ '<sup\M[^>]*>\s*(?:<a\M[^>]*>)?\s*\d{1,3}(?:\s*,\s*\d{1,3})*\s*(?:</a>)?\s*</sup>'
                     and strpos(content, '<div class="fns">') = 0) as marker_no_section
from report where report_version = 'sprs3';
```

### C16a: the newer-format sitting responses that were refetched

The stored side of the comparison. Each row's `sitting_date` was sent to `getHansardReport/` with the gateway's own pieces (`settings.hansard_report_url`, `CRAWL_HEADERS`, `send_with_retry`), the bodies were saved outside the repository, and each body's `attendanceList` was compared with `attendance_list`, both parsed as JSON.

```sql
select id, sitting_date, attendance_list
from hansardsittingdateresponse
where date_to_display is not null
order by id;
```

### C17a: speech labels whose lookup key `normalize_name` changes

```sql
select count(distinct speaker) as labels,
  count(distinct speaker) filter (where speaker ~ '\m[A-Z]\. [A-Z]\.') as labels_spaced_initials,
  count(distinct speaker) filter (where speaker ~ 'Mohd\.') as labels_mohd,
  count(*) as speech_rows,
  count(*) filter (where speaker ~ '\m[A-Z]\. [A-Z]\.') as rows_spaced_initials,
  count(*) filter (where speaker ~ 'Mohd\.') as rows_mohd
from speech where speaker is not null;
```
